| Adversarial prefix state search | Resource limit | 1,603 | 1,000,000 |

[The TypeScript measurement program](../test/research/re01/measure.ts) reproduces those deterministic counts without ignored logs. The shared-DAG terminal retains a recursive type-reference ID; this measures nonexpansion of legal element/type recursion, not an implementation of recursive payload validity. The adversarial exhaustion reports no partial successful witness and no invalidity.

The coordinator owns the final `ci`, full reference, conformance, installed-consumer and documentation/support-matrix checks on the integrated tree. Those results and the exact reviewed commit are recorded in the joint handoff; the old #231 results do not substitute. IDE inspections are unavailable in this environment; repository-local checks and independent review supply verification.

## Full proof audit after resolver delivery

This continuation starts from verified main `b0992d00768cff5891cb924a734892ea675a55b8`, tree `cec7d141dfa3b9f3b2e4d96e0f17ca9626c3a6b3`.
The owner is the current Codex coordinator; fresh independent review derives the controls separately and reviews the final artifact revision in the delivery ledger.
The new [strict reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-ambient-completeness.test.ts) contain seven invoked tests over nine new sources, without production assessment imports.

The full theorem is not discharged by the resolver or this audit.
Two proposed reductions fail on concrete examples: choosing only the smallest outgoing closure, and assigning every fresh intermediate an empty `block` set.
There is also an unresolved distinction between literal abstract extension constraints and D1's source-style AU construction algebra; a complete theorem must state its witness domain before quantifying over candidates.

### Ambient membership is not monotone

[The original source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-choice-original.xsd) declares global `h:anyType`, affiliate `m:string`, and ancestor `A` with global `h[2..2]` inside a singleton sequence.
`B` restricts `A` to two mandatory qualified local `h:anyType` declarations; `D` extends `B` vacuously.
Existing `T` extends `A` vacuously, giving the [retained proposed source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-choice-retained.xsd) a two-step construction with the same final content properties.

[Particle restriction](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-restrict) 2.2 removes `A`'s singleton `1..1` sequence for comparison.
Clause 2.1 expands the remaining global `h[2..2]` into `choice(h[1..1],m[1..1])[2..2]`, because its in-context substitution group contains `m` as well as `h`.
The final local declarations do not undergo this global-head expansion.

[MapAndSum](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-MapAndSum) maps both local members to the same synthetic `h[1..1]` member.
Each NameAndTypeOK comparison has the same QName, identical `anyType`, unit bounds, false nillability and no fixed or identity constraints; the two declarations also satisfy EDC's named-type identity requirement.
The restricted root's `1..1` bounds multiplied by its two direct members give `2..2`, exactly the base choice's range.

[The omitted source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-choice-omitted.xsd) changes only the presence of `m` in the proposed schema.
With no nonself affiliate, clause 2.1 no longer expands `h`, so the table dispatches sequence-to-element as Forbidden.
This failure affects both proposed `D` and unchanged original `B`; the resolver explicitly retains `B` as an original endpoint-chain operand.

The omitted incoming affiliate is not an outgoing target needed for structural closure.
Consequently, minimal outgoing closure need not preserve a valid component restriction, and larger closed membership need not be dispensable.
The earlier [retained-substitution control](content-model-s06-re-01-resolver.md#established-retained-substitution-negative-control) shows the opposite direction: adding a retained affiliate can invalidate proposed ancestry.

No monotone membership pruning theorem follows from structural closure.
If the witness domain permits smaller schemas, every permitted closed membership choice must be covered or replaced by a proved reduction.
The allowance and bound for fresh auxiliary globals also need definition: incoming fresh affiliates can change the same implicit-choice normalization and are not part of endpoint incidence comparison.

### A terminating negative proof for one fixed ambient requirement

In the earlier retained-substitution control, retain original `M:D` affiliated to original `H:B`, where original `B` restricts `A` and `A` has base `anyType`.
Every allowed two-step endpoint has ancestry `D -> E -> A -> anyType`, with `E` immediately extending `A`.
`E` cannot be original `B`, whose immutable method is restriction, nor can a fresh type take anchored `B`'s identity.

Thus the complex TypeDerivationOK query from proposed `D` to original `B` fails for every such intermediate, regardless of its content, AU set, wildcard or fresh auxiliary members.
[Element properties](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#e-props-correct) 4 therefore fails for retained `M/H` under that fixed ambient requirement.
This is an exhaustive identity/path obstruction, not a finite sample of failed candidates.

The proof uses only the fixed four-identity proposed ancestry and terminates without enumerating particles or occurrence counts.
It establishes nonexistence only in contexts required to retain `M/H`; the permitted omission control has no such obligation.
A configured computation still needs to pay for preparation, comparisons and its receipt; inability to pay returns resource-limit rather than this negative result.

### Fresh intermediate blocking cannot default to empty

[The block control](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-block-original.xsd) has empty `A`, `B` restricting `A` with `block="restriction"`, and `D` extending `B`.
Global `M:D` is affiliated to `H:A`, while retained `C` contains `choice(ref H,ref M)`.
The original schema has no original type immediately extending `A` that could supply a named intermediate.

[Substitution Group OK](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec) 2.3 unions the head and intermediate types' prohibited substitutions.
Original `B.block` suppresses `M` from `H`'s effective substitution set, so `C`'s mandatory alternative positions are disjoint.
The affiliation remains legal; suppression of substitutability is a different predicate.

Replacing `D` by a restriction of fresh `E` extending `A` with [empty block](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-block-empty.xsd) admits `M` into `H`'s effective substitution set and makes `C` ambiguous on `M`.
Giving `E` [restriction block](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/ambient-block-restriction.xsd) restores the original effective set and removes that ambiguity.
`block` is not `final`: this setting does not prohibit `D`'s restriction derivation.

A complete fresh-intermediate family must cover the four subsets of complex-type `block`, or prove a contextual reduction with the same results.
Enumerating only particle shapes and original immediately-extending types misses this parameter when `C` is retained.
The conditional source and relation checks do not establish that these options cover all other intermediate properties.

### Abstract AU extension and mapped inheritance have different premises

[The fixed restoration source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/abstract-fixed-restoration.xsd) declares unconstrained global integer `g`, optional `A/g` fixed to `1`, `B` prohibiting it, and final `D/g` fixed to `2`.
Each original operand is valid against the same original scalar declaration; the actual source mapping distinguishes the two AU identities.
No calendar, QName alias or scalar-order decision is needed for the exact integer inequality `1 != 2`.

D1 requires every intermediate extension to preserve `A/g`'s identity and constraint.
Its final action cannot retain that identity while producing only `D/g`; replacing it by fixed `2` fails accepted AU01's universal original-fixed predicate, and prohibition cannot yield the nonempty final set.
This is a complete negative argument for this AU projection under D1's construction algebra.

The literal [extension constraint](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-extends) 1.2 expands its word "subset" by matching declaration name, namespace and simple type.
It does not mention the AU ID, requiredness or own fixed value in that expansion.
An abstract intermediate containing only original `D/g` matches the original declaration and passes that local property condition; retaining it at the final restriction does not compare it with removed `A/g`.

[Conformance](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#concepts-conformance) permits non-XML component schemas, but does not prove that every such extension is source-style set union.
The accepted AU01 contract supplies that union for actual mapped extensions; it does not settle completeness of D1 relative to every literal abstract-component construction.
This contrast is a witness-domain obligation, not a proposal to weaken AU01 source replacement or treat conflicting runtime values as schema-invalidity.

Choosing source-style construction or literal abstract components changes the domain of the existence theorem.
The proof must justify its choice against the approved rule; an implementation can check D1 plans without establishing that D1 covers the full rule.
XML-source witnesses introduce separate fresh-AU/local-scope and anonymous-scalar correspondence obligations and cannot be substituted silently.

### Mixed empty prefixes require raw construction and normalized comparison

[The mixed original](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/mixed-empty-prefix-original.xsd) has a real empty sequence in mixed `A`; its final mixed content is exactly seventeen `a,b` pairs.
Under complex-content [mapping](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#declare-type) 2.1.4, mixed empty syntax maps to a real `1..1` empty sequence particle, rather than absent content.
The [shadow](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/mixed-empty-prefix-shadow.xsd) extends `A` by a source-valid singleton sequence containing `##any/skip[0..unbounded]`.

Its raw intermediate is `sequence(A.emptySequence,sequence(W))`, preserving the original first member as ParticleExtension requires.
Restriction normalization removes the empty prefix and pointless singleton wrappers, leaving `W`; selected PW01 checks the final group's exact `34..34` formal range against `0..unbounded`.
No required dead separator is inserted, and no raw all group is erased for extension placement.

This discharges one preparation category that source-mapped absence alone did not cover.
It does not prove the complete normalization lemma for every pointless all/choice/sequence, repeated root, mixed/simple transition or recursive identity case.
The unchanged prototype accepts already prepared comparison views; its null ancestor cannot stand in for unverified raw particle absence.

### What a complete candidate reduction still has to prove

The existing finite particle mapping and D1 AU criterion are conditional projections, not a complete full-type family.
The full family must cover original intermediate identities, permitted fresh content/flag/AU variants, every permitted ambient membership choice and every necessary fresh auxiliary component.
Anonymous identities, original owners, endpoint sharing, scalar operands and raw extension placement remain anchored constraints, rather than properties that may be discarded during reduction.

Trivial one-extension chains also need a proved vacuous/no-op case outside a cyclic endpoint overlay.
The extension rule's note explicitly covers the only-extension case, but choosing the endpoint itself as ancestor and then adding `E extends D; D restricts E` creates a prohibited base cycle.
The full theorem must cover direct ur-type and ordinary simple-builtin ancestry without treating that resolver-plan rejection as semantic nonexistence.

Soundness requires complete proposed-context component legality and exact endpoint correspondence under the selected witness domain.
Completeness requires transforming an arbitrary allowed witness into an enumerated candidate while preserving those obligations, including implicit substitution normalization, UPA/EDC and all unchanged members' derivation constraints.
The resolver's conditional test owners supply no authority for that transformation or for unimplemented semantic predicates.

Other retained extension types can themselves carry `cos-ct-extends` 1.5 obligations.
A complete argument must show those obligations transfer or provide a terminating joint treatment; recursively assuming the RE01 search succeeds is circular.
The resolver's direct predicates intentionally do not perform that recursive search.

A finite verified family and terminating qualified predicates would yield terminating negatives by complete enumeration of that family.
Enumeration of arbitrary finite graphs instead supplies positive semidecision only, unless a small-witness bound or equivalent decision theorem is proved.
Neither the node/work defaults nor a configured candidate count can supply that missing bound.

### Joint contract and diagnostic reconciliation

AU01 C1 v1, PW01 R-240 v1 and DT01 A v1 share reviewed head `c5f6e38600e7acbd700bf15eaea3092ba15fb174`, tree `28a0907ffe4ccc068197c16014c87915b00df495`.
Their normal merged acceptance is `f4e39e819f2d9aa264cfdd14d16154c6443c9bac` with the identical tree; the current manifest preserves historical pins beside current TypeScript artifact hashes.
The [AU](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/233#issuecomment-6096188422), [PW](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/235#issuecomment-6096188941) and [DT](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/236#issuecomment-6096189431) acceptance records identify the independent review and precise local scopes.

| Consumer | Original operands required | Delegated assumption still required by RE01 |
|---|---|---|
| AU01 C1 | Every original AU/declaration/type ID, own/declaration value, source role and namespace context | Prepared uses, qualified scalar derivation/equality and wildcard legality |
| PW01 R-240 | Raw and normalized particles, original member/use bounds, wildcard namespace/process/identity | Legal raw placement, UPA/EDC, certified normalization and #178 formal ranges |
| DT01 A | Original scalar identity, lexical witness, timezone, facets and member order | Full original-type assessment; calendars only within DT01's reviewed domain |
| RE01 resolver | Actual chain, anchored endpoint incidence, original source records and declared membership | Selected witness domain and all-member semantic receipts |

The new controls use identical `anyType` or exact integer facts; they do not delegate calendar behavior to an invented alternate engine.
General AU fixed/default comparison must still assess each operand under its original scalar type, whitespace, facet, list/union and QName context before invoking selected equality.
DT01's indeterminate order is not inequality or failed equality, and an invalid operand cannot be converted into distinct values.

All stages preserve independent inclusive defaults of 100,000 nodes and 1,000,000 work units, with charging before construction, indexing, fixed points, comparisons and copying.
A future integrated witness request must share its accounting across candidates and semantic owners rather than restart each candidate at the full default and call that one bounded run.
The resolver's existing request accounting and numeric boundary ledger remain valid for one candidate; they do not measure the unimplemented global search.

A rejected candidate is not witness nonexistence; a missing preparation or predicate authority is unresolved; exhausted accounting is resource-limit with no partial receipt.
Only an exhaustive proved criterion may establish an RE01 invalid-schema answer; C1 conflicting absence remains payload invalid-value under #184.
Production unsupported-capability remains #179's existing qualification boundary and cannot become a permanent exclusion for these unresolved proof cases.

Current libxml2 2.15.1 accepts all three ambient-choice sources despite the independently derived omitted-context component failure.
It accepts the AU restoration and both mixed-prefix controls, accepts the original/blocked-intermediate sources, and rejects the empty-block source.
These nine observations remain separate from the sourced clause expectations and prove no complete RE01 procedure or product capability.

## Missing proof, options and recommendation

The unresolved implementation obligation is precise: prove a terminating negative criterion for every approved complex type, including source-valid pointless normalization/all placement, mixed-to-simple and simple-content restrictions, original final/block/type identity, and AU witness equality/representation. Selected sibling predicates discharge their own interpretation questions; they do not supply that RE theorem. The finite particle family demonstrates useful constructive progress; its failure cannot yet establish that no full type witness exists.

| Option | Consequence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Formal existential component rule with complete proof | Preserves legal restorations and dead/wildcard controls | Continue this direction |
| Explanatory no-addback prohibition | Rejects known vacuous witnesses and narrows profile | Reject |
| Copy original extension suffixes only | Misses integer wildcard and pointless-prefix witnesses | Reject |
| Add payload-language inclusion to component legality | Changes the approved rule and downstream ownership | Requires explicit contract decision |
| Treat bounded failed search as invalid | Unsound negative result | Reject |
| Approve only an easier derivation profile | New permanent exclusion, not authorized here | Reject |

No maintainer preference among those alternatives can manufacture the missing completeness proof. If a rule change is considered, present its concrete compatibility effect separately; keep the affected qualification open while continuing the already valid positive evidence and sibling research.

## Exact #179 integration handoff

Keep `checkReorderedDerivation` guarded until the full proof and sibling predicate assumptions are reviewed. Then replace its ancestor-scan refusal with a pure complete witness decision fed by the existing `prepareResolvedCompilationInput` and the single accepted occurrence analysis. Validate every original derivation and retain all original operands before attempting reordering.

Integrate the reviewed component normalization/prefix extraction and certified candidate construction into `particleSchemaAssessment`, rather than creating another occurrence or payload engine. Feed original AU sets and exact typed fixed/default predicates through the existing attribute/scalar owners; validate hypothetical intermediate component constraints and the final complete restriction. Charge every preparation, pair state and copy before allocation.

Add regressions for all eleven fixture contrasts, required empty choice versus optional/absent choice, multiple unrelated suffix members, all-root vacuity and nonvacuous limitations, mixed/simple transitions, final/block preservation, fixed/default alias contexts and multiple matching original AUs. Include recursive type references, shared DAGs, huge exact repetitions and at/beyond-budget request-wide failure without partial operations.

Only a complete reviewed type-level result can discharge `S06-RE-01` for an affected operation. Complete RE01 acceptance, full production #179 tests and combined #153 acceptance remain distinct gates; this research record changes no public support claim.

## Executed design investigation for #260

This continuation executes [#260](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/260), rather than accepting the complete #234 theorem.
The coordinating author is Codex `/root`; the separate nonauthor reviewer is `/root/independent_review`.
The starting revision is freshly fetched main `9e3a22f6b494fff19e08d606486d54e55f19a5e7`, tree `37ae20f143e3e73af8980474192cf3259c2145cf`.

### Task 1: Current evidence inventory

The latest [#234 audit handoff](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6103550655) and [#232 joint status](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/232#issuecomment-6103555825) qualify this baseline.
The unaccepted #231 draft remains `460f5b8379c68ffef79917284e445b5ab046429e`; it was inspected separately and contributes no executable dependency.
Current instructions require strict TypeScript, Node 24 or 26 and the Node reference lane.

| Question | Present conclusion | Classification | Concrete evidence | Domain and dependency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| May a removed optional field return? | A vacuous witness can permit it | Established scoped contrast | [Original contract controls](../test/conformance/reference/s06-re01-contract.test.ts) | Numbered existential rule; complete local restriction |
| Does message-language inclusion decide RE01? | It does not replace formal mapping | Refuted substitution | Required-empty-choice controls above | Component rules versus payload languages |
| Can extension suffix copying cover witnesses? | Universal wildcard constructions defeat it | Refuted family | [Witness probe](../test/research/re01/witness-probe.test.ts) | Qualified particle preparation/predicates |
| Can normalized content replace raw construction? | Raw all placement still matters | Refuted shortcut | [Particle continuation](../test/conformance/reference/re01-particle-continuation.test.ts) | Restriction-only pointlessness |
| Are equal-looking intermediates interchangeable? | Original T can succeed where a copy fails | Refuted identity reduction | [Endpoint controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-endpoint-continuation.test.ts) | Anchored local type relation, E2 context |
| Does deleting ambient membership only help? | It can enable or disable formal validity | Refuted monotonicity | [Ambient controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-ambient-completeness.test.ts) | Explicit retained declaration universe |
| Can fresh block default to empty? | A nonempty block can prevent UPA overlap | Refuted simplification | Ambient controls and new experiment below | Block affects substitution, not derivation final |
| Does D1 represent every abstract extension? | Literal declaration matching is weaker | Open equivalence | Fixed-restoration control and clause 1.2 | Original AU IDs, requiredness and fixed operands |
| Does one checked candidate decide existence? | It checks only its declared context/predicates | Established limited guarantee | [Resolver implementation](content-model-s06-re-01-resolver-implementation.md) | Six qualified owners; no full schema engine |
| Are sibling local meanings accepted? | AU C1, PW R-240, DT A remain accepted | Established local contracts | [Joint contract map](content-model-s06-research-handoff.md#four-rule-contract-map) | Original operands and reviewed prerequisites |
| Can retained predicates transfer unchanged? | Context-sensitive ones require rechecking | Conditional transfer only | Resolver validation/relations controls | Changed composition, substitution and endpoint |
| Is there an unrestricted finite witness bound? | No such bound is established here | Open theorem | Earlier audit plus Task 6 below | All content categories, helpers and identities |
| Are resource limits a witness bound? | They bound execution only | Refuted negative inference | Resolver ledger and finite shared-run control | Charge preparation, checking and output |

The genuine unresolved questions are witness-domain quantification, endpoint correspondence, complete legality/normalization, retained-obligation transfer and an exhaustive terminating negative method.
More engine agreement or a larger arbitrary graph cutoff cannot settle those questions.
The new records preserve established, conditional, refuted and open claims separately.

### Task 2: Construction and preservation decision packet

The pinned complete structures XML was freshly retrieved and reproduced SHA-256 `e496af408b14853e6169ac7c1fca09d55a81bd73771758ad6be020960e1317ba`.
Final clauses were read with deleted editorial text excluded.
The [source-access record](../test/research/re01/decision-sources.json) identifies external sources, access scope and limitations.

The [published second-edition errata](https://www.w3.org/2004/03/xmlschema-errata) were freshly read without extraction truncation.
Their Part 1 entry E1-56 concerns default-value/PSVI contributions; it supplies no new RE01 quantifier, construction equivalence or negative theorem.
The reviewed PW01 WG proposal remains distinct from an adopted correction.

`concepts-conformance` permits dynamically constructed non-XML schemas; `conformance-schemaValidity` still requires all applicable component constraints recursively.
`layer1` permits lazy assembly but requires established definitions/declarations to remain unchanged and acquisition to preserve assessment results.
These clauses describe assessment and representation; they do not specify which original ambient components clause 1.5's hypothetical reconstruction may omit or add.

`cos-ct-extends.1.2` expands its subset condition by matching declaration name, namespace and simple type, without explicitly comparing AU identity, requiredness or own fixed value.
XML extension mapping retains inherited uses; AU01 C1 governs actual source contributions and replacement checks.
The two rules do not alone prove that every allowed abstract extension is D1's original-use set union.

The mapping introduction also says the abstract correspondence can be constructed from the normative XML representation mapping.
That is relevant evidence for source constructions, but supplies no demonstrated graph-wide surjectivity preserving original anonymous identities, group sharing, local owners and source roles.
Likewise `scIntro`'s named equality and `no-identity`'s explicit incomplete identity definition do not select arbitrary property equality for anonymous components.

| Axis | Alternatives | Concrete compatibility consequence | Exact unresolved obligation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ambient membership | Entire original schema; permitted closed sub-schema; additional fresh incoming components | Retained/omitted choice dispatch differs; retained C can force failure | Define allowed membership and required original incoming relationships |
| AU construction algebra | D1 source-style original-use union; literal component property constraints; XML source witness | Fixed 7 to 8 fails D1 replacement; literal match omits that test | Prove equivalence or adopt and justify the intended construction algebra |
| Endpoint correspondence | Anchored non-derivation identity/incidence; explicitly permitted fresh correspondence | Group-owned uses and anonymous/local types may distinguish them | Define total comparison closure, scopes and sharing before reduction |
| Recursive context | E2 proposed queries with actual facts accessible separately | Named T works where actual-query or a fresh copy fails | Prove endpoint/context convention matches the selected existential rule |

The fixed-value comparison is local compatibility evidence, not a globally certified literal-component witness.
A declared membership policy must also decide whether a fresh affiliate can change an otherwise singleton head's restriction dispatch.
Selecting a pair of policy options would not establish endpoint soundness, abstract legality or completeness.

The defensible working model for experiments is the delivered resolver model: immutable actual graph, actual ancestor selection, original anchors, one proposed endpoint replacement and E2 queries in an explicit closed context.
Fresh components have disjoint identities; existing named intermediates remain unchanged; named and anonymous originals retain owners, sharing, lexical namespaces and original scalar operands.
D1 hypothetical retain/replace/prohibit roles never rewrite actual source roles, and XML proposals additionally require representation legality.

Trivial chains need an explicit no-op certificate rather than making the endpoint its own proposed descendant.
Simple-content chains can select `anySimpleType` through the original builtin chain; their original named scalar ancestry must still be checked.
Neither case may turn a malformed cyclic overlay into a universal negative.

Published work was screened for applicability.
Gelade, Martens and Neven's [ICDT 2007 paper preview](https://page-one.springer.com/pdf/preview/10.1007/11965893_19) discusses language inclusion/equivalence for regular-expression grammars and single-type EDTDs with numerical constraints/interleaving.
Only its abstract/introduction were available; neither that abstraction nor this inspection establishes the anchored component reconstruction theorem.

The full preprint extraction exceeded the tool's request budget, and two publication endpoints returned security pages.
[XMLmind's implementation limitations](https://www.xmlmind.com/xmleditor/_distrib/doc/xmltool/xsd_structure_limitations.html) explicitly list RE01 and UPA as unchecked; such processor acceptance cannot supply a theorem.
This is a bounded literature inspection, not an exhaustive claim that no applicable published result exists.

Task 2's packet is concrete, but selection remains blocked by the ambient/AU/correspondence questions.
The smallest next investigation is a clause-level construction/identity argument covering the fixed-restoration and retained/omitted controls together, including contrary XML-mapping evidence.
If evidence cannot settle that argument, the maintainer must decide the explicitly documented witness contract and compatibility consequences before #234 proves that contract.

### Task 3: Complete proposal checking flow and owners

The flow is actual input qualification, original ancestor selection, permitted construction, explicit membership/closure, raw construction legality, proposed relations, certified comparison normalization, anchored endpoint correspondence and all-member semantic checks.
Every applicable check must pass with a named qualified authority before a complete positive receipt can be issued.
A failed proposal rejects that proposal; missing authority is unresolved.

| Boundary | Original or constructed inputs | Present guarantee | Missing semantic authority or proof owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Preparation | Actual IDs, ownership, all references and source contributions | Structural closure, prohibited cycles, immutable actual records | #234 permitted witness domain; #179 complete actual adapter |
| Type construction | Original raw base/content/final; proposed base/method/content | Direct relation/final checks; owner request | #234 every content transition, trivial forms and retained RE obligations |
| Attributes | Original AU/declaration IDs, own/declaration constraints, source local/group roles | D1 bookkeeping and AU C1 original matching | Prepared scalar qualification, wildcard admission and abstract-equivalence proof |
| Particle restriction | Raw/effective roots, original group/use/member bounds and wildcard processing | Qualified owner request; conditional particle search | Full dispatch, raw placement, certified normalization; #178 formal range inputs |
| Attribution | Ordered use positions and proposed implicit substitution members | Context-qualified position inventory | Complete UPA, including repeated/grouped and recursive context consequences |
| Declarations | Anchored named/local declarations, types, scope and identity constraints | Qualified owner request; relation subchecks | EDC and all declaration/component constraints omitted from narrow Facts |
| Scalars | Original lexical/type/facet/list/union/namespace context for each operand | Owner inventory; DT A only in its calendar domain | Full original-type assessment, QName/facet validity and typed equality |
| Endpoint | Actual/proposed non-derivation closure and supplied incidence certificate | Anchored identity/property comparison | #234 justification of the required closure and permitted correspondence |
| Retained members | Every proposed member, including unchanged/source-only records | Conservative all-member obligation enumeration | Transfer lemma or terminating joint handling of retained RE01 obligations |

`resolver-validation.ts` passes raw declared and effective roots separately, preserves original source operands, and invokes six direct owners.
Its qualified receipts authenticate the supplied authority/context; conditional test owners only validate orchestration.
The seven-builtin ancestry table is not a complete scalar, facet or content checker.

Representation facts can transfer when their original source/namespace operands remain identical.
Scalar validity can transfer only under unchanged original type and namespace/facet context with a qualified receipt.
An unchanged declaration or particle's context-sensitive validity cannot transfer merely because its bytes are unchanged: new endpoint ancestry changes substitutions, dispatch, UPA/EDC and composition.

The new missing-qualification control obtains successful structural/relational preparation and still returns `unresolved: missing-qualified-authority` with source context.
This protects the checking boundary from accidentally promoting the finite experiment into complete schema validity.
No #231 assessment module is imported.

### Task 4: Competing method packet

The finite family F0 combines the existing qualified particle constructions with only fresh intermediates and empty block.
The revised research family F1 considers every relevant unchanged original intermediate, all four fresh complex block subsets, explicit legal membership and separately certified particle/AU/content constructions.
F1 remains a conjectural full family: neither fresh helper bounds nor normalization/context reduction is proved.

```text
qualify original graph, source operands and selected witness contract
apply proved necessary impossibility criteria in their exact domains
construct original intermediates and certified fresh standard forms
for each permitted flag/context/construction choice using one request budget:
    reject malformed closure separately from semantic candidate failure
    check raw construction, endpoint correspondence and every context member
    return yes only with a complete qualified construction
return no only with a reviewed exhaustive-family certificate
otherwise return the named unresolved obligation or resource-limit
```

The independent bounded constraint model uses five intermediate variants: unchanged empty-block T and four fresh block subsets.
Two membership bits record affiliate M and choice C; closure requires C to imply M because C refers to M.
An ancestry-only projection, an H:A blocking projection and their H:T joint projection are evaluated separately.

| Method | Positive evidence | Negative meaning | Proof obligations and expected cost | Small falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 standard forms | Fully qualified checked construction | No only after arbitrary-witness reduction | Original identities, flags, helpers, all content/ambient cases; mapping polynomial locally, contexts potentially exponential | Successful allowed construction with no preserving standard form |
| Bounded constraints | Assignment plus separately checked predicates | Unsat only in the encoded finite projection | Faithful encoding and domain/small-witness theorem; exhaustive finite product here | Independent row or checker disagreeing with encoded clauses |
| Direct criteria | Construction satisfying jointly sufficient stated conditions | Entire permitted class ruled out by a necessary condition | Joint sufficiency and complete case classification; short ancestry paths here | Locally passing predicates whose combination is impossible |

The constraint model is plain strict TypeScript Boolean enumeration; no solver dependency is selected.
Its bounded positive result remains a projection, not a complete component construction.
Direct ancestry arguments are retained as explanation-producing early exits rather than silently promoted to a complete general criterion.

### Task 5: Executed comparative experiments

[The executable finite model](../test/research/re01/decision-experiment.ts) and [independent controls](../test/research/re01/decision-experiment.test.ts) enumerate 20 assignments for each of 12 query settings.
Five assignments per query violate C-to-M closure and are recorded as outside-context, rather than semantic schema failures.
The remaining assignments cover every combination of the declared intermediate, flag and membership variables.

| Query | Constraint-admitted assignments | F0 admitted assignments | Independent direct answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| H:A blocking, M and C required | 3 | 0 | Some nonempty fresh block exists |
| Anchored T ancestry only, M required and C optional | 2 | 0 | Original T satisfies the projection |
| Anchored T plus UPA, M and C required | 0 | 0 | Fixed retained context is impossible |
| Anchored T plus UPA, M required and C optional | 1 | 0 | Original T works after omitting C in this projection |

These counts reproduce with [decision-measure.ts](../test/research/re01/decision-measure.ts).
All 12 bounded constraint verdicts agree with independently derived direct answers; F0 misses the blocking and identity positives.
F1's identity/flag variants cover this finite projection only, not unrestricted RE01.

The independent reviewer derived the block truth table from `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec.2.3` and ran the relation checker separately.
For proposed D restricting fresh E extending A, both derivation methods occur, so each nonempty E block suppresses M without invalidating affiliation.
The tests vary incidental names while preserving symbol references and prove the same relation behavior under renaming.

The new combined example preserves original T and H:T, M:D affiliated to H, and C's two distinct choice positions H/M.
Actual B restricts T with `block=extension`; actual D extends B, so original affiliation remains legal while B prevents overlap in C.
The original's complete RE01 status is the question under investigation, not an assumed valid premise.

The meaningful two-member raw all in A/T forces a vacuous-content intermediate by `cos-all-limited`.
Every final all member requires a complete mapping, even though its XML occurrence is optional; the obligatory n:D-to-n:T pair excludes extension/list/union.
Only unchanged original T can appear on the required D-to-E-to-A base path and satisfy that anchored comparison.

With E=T, B's blocking edge disappears and M becomes substitutable for H; C's two positions overlap, violating UPA.
Any fresh disjoint E immediately based on A cannot reach anchored T, regardless of added helpers, content, final or block.
Fresh incoming affiliates cannot remove the existing H/M overlap, so this is an exhaustive scoped identity/context obstruction, not just five rejected proposals.

[Three new source fixtures](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/combined-identity-original.xsd) and [source/reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-decision-investigation.test.ts) preserve the raw all, original ancestry, block and retained declarations.
Libxml2 2.15.1 accepts the original source and rejects both the reused-T and blocked-copy sources.
Those engine observations are recorded separately from the primary-clause argument and do not select the ambient or abstract witness contract.

The simple-content control starts with actual D-to-A-to-string ancestry and retained H:string/M:D.
Current ancestor selection reaches `anySimpleType`; proposed D-to-E-to-anySimpleType loses the anchored string relation.
This establishes a relation obstruction for that supplied proposal, without claiming complete simple-content formation or a full-domain negative.

AU controls parameterize valid equal/distinct integer values, QName aliases and conflicting original namespace bindings.
D1 extension retains original uses; replacement checks every original typed fixed operand, while retention from a different literal-model base has different premises.
An invalid original integer remains `integer-operand` invalidity instead of distinct valid values; no general scalar qualification is inferred.

Existing raw singleton-all, nontransitive restriction, mixed empty-prefix, repeated-root, named-group, recursive endpoint and empty-choice controls were rerun.
They remain independent normative/selected/conditional/engine evidence categories.
No transformation that drops declarations, copies identities or erases raw wrappers is assumed harmless.

The finite counterexample enumerator uses the existing sticky request budget and reserves assignment nodes before construction.
One batch shares accounting for all 12 queries and result copying, requiring 68,384 work units and 240 assignment nodes under the default limits.
It measures its own finite transition/copy model, not the resolver, WASM memory or an implemented global search.

At the measured inclusive full-run limit it succeeds; one less returns resource-limit with no accumulated receipt.
Exhaustion with positive remaining allowance permanently stops later candidate construction, and 20/19 node controls admit/reject the exact 20-assignment domain.
The batch exposes no partial rows if a later query or final copy exhausts the shared allowance.

### Task 6: Coverage, termination and joint assumptions

The attempted reduction fails at three independently observed boundaries: F0 loses original T, empty-block-only loses contextual UPA repairs, and outgoing-only loses enabling substitution dispatch.
Combining an original-identity solution with a fresh-block solution is invalid when their joint constraints require mutually incompatible intermediates.
A complete reduction must preserve all these relationships simultaneously.

| Claim | Investigation outcome | Remaining proof or next action |
|---|---|---|
| Ordered prefix mapping terminates | Established under terminating qualified pair predicates | At most (r+1)(b+1) states; no repetition expansion |
| Every witness reduces to F1 | Open | Transform arbitrary context, identities, flags and helper graph preserving all legality |
| D1 equals literal abstract extension | Open, local premises differ | Resolve Task 2 algebra and full endpoint identity model |
| Simplified comparison certifies raw placement | Contradicted | Separate raw/source certificate and normalization lemma for all categories |
| Scoped anchored-T joint negative | Established under fixed context/identity premises | Do not extrapolate to permitted omissions or a different endpoint contract |
| All retained validity transfers | Contradicted for relations/UPA | Recheck changed contexts; prove RE01 transfer or terminating joint measure |
| Finite graph enumeration yields general no | Unproved | Small-witness bound or equivalent complete decision theorem |
| Request budget supplies solution-space bound | False | Inclusive execution accounting only; no semantic nonexistence |
| Local contracts compose automatically | Unproved | Establish each original-operand/preparation predicate before invoking it |

For F1, unchanged original intermediate count is finite and four complex block subsets are finite.
Context membership alone can require up to 2^n original subsets before closure pruning; neither relevant-subset reduction nor fresh-helper count is proved.
The number of helpers/anonymous components is not bounded by the configured node or work limit.

Existing D1 AU synthesis is conditional on prepared original sets and qualified scalar predicates.
PW R-240 retains both original member bounds and the formal whole-group range supplied by #178; adjusted member bounds cannot replace the whole-group operands.
DT A supplies its selected calendar meaning only after original type/facet/lexical/QName qualification, including indeterminate ordering as a separate result.

The required result distinctions are checked-candidate, rejected-candidate, malformed/outside-context, missing qualification, invalid original scalar, indeterminate comparison, resource-limit and exhaustively proved nonexistence.
Only the last can justify a universal RE01 negative.
AU C1 absent conflicting augmentation remains payload invalid-value owned by #184.

### Task 7: Engineering recommendation and precise handoff

Advance F1 as the next proof candidate after resolving Task 2; retain bounded constraints as its independent counterexample oracle and direct impossibility criteria as scoped early exits.
The experiments support that research architecture and reject F0's fresh/empty-only family, monotone context pruning and independent local-predicate sufficiency.
They do not justify selecting any complete RE01 algorithm or accepting D1 as the unrestricted construction domain.

Use the immutable actual graph and proposed resolver overlay as the shared checking seam.
Separate units should qualify actual operands, certify raw construction/normalization, construct F1 candidates, verify endpoint correspondence, check all context members and combine request-wide results.
Every candidate generator and semantic owner must use the same request budget, with charging before preparation, construction, checking and copying.

The delivered resolver currently creates its request budget for one supplied candidate.
An integrated multi-candidate search still needs a reviewed shared-budget preparation/checking interface, cumulative identity accounting, cache isolation and cancellation without partial success.
The finite enumerator's shared-run evidence does not implement that interface.

Production support, defaults, catalog formats, guarded paths and #231 remain unchanged.
Final algorithm selection is blocked until the construction contract supports its completeness premises.

## Construction contract and engineering recommendation after #260 continuation

This continuation starts from normally merged `f52d621e291c60a3220ec8457d110e42d83092bf`, tree `8a6ee8ed2193185139e967de2ef6bfc1db1a046c`.
It supersedes the preceding investigation's unresolved construction-model and recommendation disposition; the experiments and limitations at that checkpoint remain historical evidence.
The research recommendation is a literal abstract component graph with an explicit preserved assessment context, one coherent hypothetical endpoint and a shared qualified checker.

### Research completion and theorem acceptance

Task 7 asks for a defensible procedure or architecture and actionable owner interfaces, rather than an already proved total decision algorithm.
The preceding phrase “unconditional complete-method selection” imposed a stronger gate than the [execution assignment](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/260#issuecomment-6107368413).
The complete arbitrary-witness reduction, general negative answers and mathematical termination remain #234's acceptance obligations.

Task 2 still requires a defensible permitted-construction model.
The model below makes the preserved context an explicit input and removes the unjustified XML-style AU algebra; it does not claim that clause 1.5 explicitly quantifies every schema root.
The issue's whole-context preservation requirement supplies that boundary, supported by component closure and the existing incoming-reference contrasts.

### Literal component graph domain

Let `Γ` be the immutable, explicitly supplied whole original assessment schema, including every root already in that schema, its component-property closure and the relation-sensitive declarations supplied to assessment.
It is an input established before search, not a subset variable that the candidate generator may prune.
If an application supplies a different assessment schema, it has supplied a different problem; the previous retained/omitted contrasts demonstrate why their answers cannot be merged.

The dated [component model](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#scIntro) defines properties as a graph and permits re-entrant component references.
[Schema validity](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#conformance-schemaValidity) requires the schema's recursively included components to satisfy their constraints.
These clauses justify closure and coherent definitions once `Γ` is specified, rather than a theorem that local valid derivation alone requires retaining every available source document.

An admissible proposal contains all required original components, the proposed endpoint and a finite set of additions.
Additions may include complex/simple types, particles, groups, declarations, attribute uses, scalar/facet helpers and incoming affiliations, with any property values permitted by the component definitions.
Their legality, ownership, namespace/name uniqueness, closure and effects on every included component must be checked; there is no assumed helper-count or occurrence-size cutoff.

Builtins retain their specified identities and definitions.
The relevant ancestor is selected from the actual derivation chain exactly as clause 1.5 specifies: the ancestor whose base is the ur-type, with qualified builtin/simple ancestry included.
It is not silently replaced by the nearest complex ancestor; the existing simple-content contrast demonstrates why that substitution is unsafe.

The intermediate may be an unchanged original component with the required identity, or a fresh legal component.
It must support extension from that actual ancestor and restriction to the proposed endpoint, including the permitted primitive vacuous cases.
Source contributions constrain actual XML-derived operands; retain/replace/prohibit flags do not define all possible abstract intermediate properties.

### Endpoint identity and preservation

The proposed endpoint retains the original endpoint's identity and all non-derivation property values, component references, scalar/facet contexts and sharing.
Only its immediate base and derivation method are replaced for the hypothetical restriction; original provenance remains available in the immutable actual graph.
Properties such as final, block, abstract, content category and value constraints are preserved even when they influence downstream comparisons.

Every other required original component retains its property values and references.
An original reference to the endpoint resolves to the single proposed endpoint in the hypothetical graph, so its relationship-dependent answers may change.
Those changed answers require rechecking; immutable property preservation alone does not transfer semantic validity.

Named originals preserve their expanded names within symbol spaces.
The component model forbids simultaneous same-name copies, so a named endpoint cannot be a restriction for one comparison and its old extension for a recursive comparison in that same proposed graph.
This supports E2's coherent overlay and rejects E1's mixed resolution as a construction of one schema.

Anonymous originals, local declarations and utility components are reused with their original identity and ownership.
[Identity caveats](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#no-identity) permit implementations to differ in incompletely specified cases; this contract chooses reuse and makes that project preservation policy explicit.
Fresh helpers cannot claim an original identity, and no unnamed-copy equivalence or source reconstruction theorem is assumed.

The contract preserves the desired component's property graph directly; it does not require regenerating an equivalent XML document.
Whether a proposed standard-form reduction can replace helpers by equivalent copies while preserving every relevant predicate is a #234 proof obligation against this declared model.
Existing original intermediate identities remain first-class candidates because the all-group control refutes fresh-copy substitution.

### Why XML-style AU inheritance is insufficient

[Conformance](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#concepts-conformance) separates abstract component constraints from XML representation and mapping requirements.
Consequently, a hypothetical abstract intermediate need not be the source-mapped union of original base attribute uses.
It must satisfy the actual extension predicate and all other applicable component constraints.

The specific expansion in [extension clause 1.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-extends) compares attribute declaration name, namespace and type definition.
It does not additionally require equality of attribute-use identity, requiredness or fixed value.
Reading “subset” as an extra object-identity conjunct would introduce a requirement absent from that expansion; this continuation adopts the specific expanded predicate.

There is contrary explanatory language: [Type Definition Hierarchy](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#Type_Derivation) describes extension as adding content, and the clause 1.5 note recommends reordered extensions.
Those descriptions explain the intended construction but do not specify an additional fixed-value error predicate; the explicit valid-extension definition refers to the numbered constraint.
Ordinary XML inheritance remains a sufficient construction recipe where its premises hold, rather than a necessary characterization of every abstract witness.

The official [XSD 1.1 comparison with 1.0](https://www.w3.org/TR/xmlschema11-1/structures.diff-1.0.html#cos-ct-extends) replaces clause 1.2's QName/type expansion with recursively identical AU properties.
That later strengthening would reject the changed-use construction; it is an edition contrast, not an adopted correction to the dated 1.0 rule used here.
Clause 1.5 remains present in that later edition.

The official [Recommendation comments inventory](https://www.w3.org/2001/05/xmlschema-rec-comments.html), R-213, reports a formal attribute restriction that conflicts with the introductory language-inclusion description.
R-243 reports tension between clause 1.5 and its no-restoration note, including an unchanged-original-only proposed repair.
These entries are marked New/Unclassified with no adopted resolution shown; they expose contrary intentions rather than settle this abstract construction or supply an extra normative error predicate.

Only those public inventory entries were read; their full originating threads and member discussions were not verified.
The model is the documented component-predicate reading with an explicit preservation policy, not a claim of settled WG intent for every ambiguous phrase.

This distinction changes outcomes without reopening AU01 C1 or its accepted universal source-replacement contract.
Those contracts continue to consume complete original AU sets and typed operands when their operations apply.
They must not be used to insert source-inherited uses into a hypothetical graph that does not contain them.

The existing [resolver preparation](../test/research/re01/resolver-context.ts) still validates D1 plans by requiring all ancestor AU identities to be retained, and accepts candidate-selected original membership.
It therefore does not implement the new fixed-`Γ` literal domain and can reject `E = {u8}` before the component predicate is reached.
The next adapter must confine that inheritance invariant to source-style plans, add literal AU-property input and enforce retention of every original root in the supplied `Γ` for this contract.

Existing resolver results remain qualified by their historical D1/membership domain.
The new narrow probe establishes the clause boundary, while the new complete adapter and six-owner checker remain #179 implementation units after #234/#232 accept their interfaces.
This continuation does not claim that the old resolver has acquired unrestricted graph support.

### Complete minimal fixed-value contrast

Take one original global `g:xs:int` declaration with no declaration-level fixed value.
User-defined types have empty content, absent attribute wildcards, empty final/block sets and ordinary valid property ranges.
`A` restricts `anyType` and has optional original use `u7` fixed to 7; `B` restricts `A` and removes that optional use; actual `D` extends `B` with original optional use `u8` fixed to 8.

Construct fresh `E` extending original `A`, with its attribute-use set containing original `u8` alone, and make proposed `D` restrict `E` while retaining `u8`.
All original declarations, `A`, `B`, the endpoint identity and the root declaration remain present.
No XML inheritance mapping is asserted for `E`.

| Applicable obligation | Independent reason |
|---|---|
| Schema/name/property closure | Unique named types; complete original references |
| Complex-type properties | Legal ranges; all bases reach `anyType`; one non-ID declaration |
| Global attribute declaration | Legal name/namespace; qualified builtin `xs:int`; no fixed value |
| Attribute-use correctness | Boolean requiredness; valid integer values; no declaration-fixed conflict |
| `A` restriction | Ur-type wildcard admits `g`; ur-type content shortcut permits empty |
| `B` restriction | Optional `g` may be omitted; empty restricts empty |
| `E` extension | Same declaration QName/type; empty extends empty; no final/wildcard hurdle |
| `E` clause 1.5 | Primitive possibly-vacuous two-step case; its only extension follows `A` |
| Proposed `D` restriction | Identical `u8`, type and fixed value; empty restricts empty |
| Root and incoming relationships | Preserved ordinary root; no user-defined affiliations or identity constraints |

The complete specified builtin environment is a qualified premise, including `anyType`'s mixed particle/wildcards, builtin datatype ancestry and the four mandatory `xsi` declarations with their scalar closure.
There are no user-defined particles, wildcards or groups in this contrast.
The primitive discharge creates no self-loop base edge and does not recursively assume that an unfinished general RE01 search succeeds.

The ordinary root satisfies element-property ranges, has no value constraint or substitution affiliation, and has an empty identity-constraint set.
Its unchanged type reference resolves proposed `D` coherently, without requiring an instance observation as a schema-validity premise.

Thus the literal component constraints admit this complete minimal abstract construction.
A source-style intermediate retaining both `u7` and `u8` rejects the final restriction to `u8`, because universal matching compares 8 with original fixed 7.
This refutes the claim that that source-union algebra characterizes every witness in the selected abstract domain.

The existing [abstract restoration source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/abstract-fixed-restoration.xsd) supplies the corresponding actual pattern using fixed 1/2.
Its primary-engine observation remains separate from this component argument.
The [new predicate](../test/research/re01/literal-attribute-extension.ts) executes only the expanded clause 1.2 projection; its result does not represent a reusable full schema-validity checker.

The [nine behavioral controls](../test/research/re01/literal-attribute-extension.test.ts) vary original identity spelling, requiredness, namespace and scalar identity, qualify an invalid original operand before replacement, and distinguish source-union rejection from literal matching.
They also exercise a shared sticky request budget at the measured inclusive boundary and reject partial conclusions after exhaustion.
They preserve original operands and use independent clause premises rather than payload snapshots or helper-call counts.

```bash
npx vitest run test/research/re01/literal-attribute-extension.test.ts
npm run typecheck:research
```

The nine controls and strict research typechecks passed on Node 24.19.0, npm 11.9.0, TypeScript 6.0.3 and Vitest 5.0.3.
The original 12-row finite report is unchanged and remains reproducible with its documented command.
The new controls establish their declared predicate boundary; the complete minimal certificate above is a clause argument reviewed separately.

### Selected engineering architecture

Build one proposal checker around immutable actual `Γ`, a coherent proposed overlay and qualified semantic owners.
The checker first validates inputs and preservation, then checks raw construction, both derivation steps, scalar operands and every affected component relationship.
Its positive result includes the construction and all qualifications; missing authority remains unresolved, rather than becoming either validity or nonexistence.

Up-front qualification covers original identity, representation, structural and scalar prerequisites, not the target RE01 existential or dependent validity that search is establishing.
Those target-dependent obligations require checked construction/certificate evidence or a justified joint proof; assuming complete actual-schema validity here would make the recommendation circular.

Generate candidates in the literal graph domain, prioritizing original identities, relevant finite flags and certified raw content/AU constructions.
Treat F1 as a proposed acceleration family whose completeness must be proved against this domain, rather than retaining its historical D1 source-union premise.
Keep a deliberately bounded independent constraint enumerator to falsify reductions and direct criteria for individually proved impossibility cases.

Fair enumeration of finite serialized graphs and certificates is a specification baseline for possible additions, not a promised terminating implementation.
With a complete qualified checker, it can establish a found positive; no finite exhaustion proves nonexistence without a separately justified coverage bound or direct theorem.
Retained RE01 obligations need well-founded certificates or a justified joint method before this baseline becomes a complete semidecision claim.

Use dovetailing for unfinished checking or enumerate finite certificates with a terminating verifier; a preferred family cannot indefinitely starve other candidates.
Coverage of finite encodings is distinct from adequacy of those certificates and termination of their verification, which #234/#232 must establish.

```text
qualify actual identity/representation/structural/scalar prerequisites and context Γ
retain target/dependent RE01 validity as obligations to establish
select actual ancestor; establish preservation obligations
for each permitted candidate considered within the shared request budget:
    prepare coherent proposed overlay without pruning Γ
    check preservation, raw legality and qualified semantic obligations
    return established positive only with a complete construction certificate
return resource-limit if accounting is exhausted
return unresolved if qualification or proof coverage is missing
return established negative only under an accepted exhaustive/direct theorem
```

Preparation, allocation, comparison, checking, rejection and copying share one request budget, with charges before work or allocation.
Cache identities include context and qualified operands; diagnostics retain the original source and hypothetical relation that failed.
Candidate rejection, original invalid-schema, scalar indeterminacy, unsupported qualification, resource-limit and proved nonexistence are separate outcomes.

| Alternative | Disposition and concrete reason |
|---|---|
| Fresh/empty-block-only F0 | Rejected; recorded identity/blocking controls miss positives |
| XML/source-union-only witness algebra | Rejected as complete abstract domain; minimal fixed contrast differs |
| E1 actual queries inside proposed graph | Rejected for coherent named endpoint; mixes two definitions |
| Unproved context deletion | Rejected; affiliation/dispatch and combined UPA controls change answers |
| Original-aware F1 | Prioritized proof candidate; raw/AU/helper/context reduction remains required |
| Bounded constraints | Retained independent falsification tool; bounds qualify every negative |
| Direct conditions | Retained scoped early exits; joint sufficiency needs proof |
| New general solver dependency | Deferred; no inspected result covers this complete component domain |

The prior language/grammar literature evidence does not supply anchored identity, recursive relationships or whole-context construction coverage.
Additional [WG discussion of combined derivation, LC-152](https://www.w3.org/2000/05/12-xmlschema-lcissues.html) explains the historical difficulty of checking simultaneous operations, without furnishing a decision theorem for clause 1.5.
Michael Kay's [implementation discussion](https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/xmlschema-dev/2006Nov/0023.html) describes group-reference identity affecting UPA and explicitly acknowledges identity ambiguity; it is explanatory implementation evidence, not adopted XSD 1.0 text.

The selected architecture changes no production support, defaults, catalog or public capability claim.

### Continuation review and closure assessment

The separate nonauthor reviewer independently challenged the subset expansion, introductory extension language, XML mapping, endpoint identity and full minimal constraint inventory.
The review corrected the earlier extra theorem gate and required the context policy to be identified as the assignment's preservation contract rather than an explicit clause 1.5 quantifier.
It also required qualifying builtin `anyType`'s closure and treating the trivial case as a primitive certificate without a derivation self-loop.

The construction model and engineering recommendation now have concrete outputs; #234 still owns their complete reduction and theorem acceptance.

The issue handoff must record those actual revisions, test results, qualifications and owner links before marking Tasks 2/7/8 complete and closing.
#234/#232/#179/#153 retain their separate proof, joint acceptance and production gates after this design investigation.

## Literal preparation and fresh attribute helper continuation

This implementing continuation starts at fetched main `3434b4c5bf1914cb2972efe589200160801bbe0a`, tree `2150bace3322b0ccebe04076b9b2b2df8966f1b8`.
Codex `/root` owns implementation and delivery, `/root/behavioral_spec` independently specifies semantic contrasts, and `/root/independent_review` reviews work it did not author.
The full R1–R6 assignment remains subject to its complete-proof gate; the concrete outputs below support unaffected delivery when that gate fails.

### R1: Domain and pinned authorities

The selected problem is the literal graph domain in the [accepted construction contract](#literal-component-graph-domain), including every component of immutable supplied Γ, exact original anonymous/local/shared identities, all non-derivation properties and one coherent endpoint.
Legal additions have no assumed cardinality, occurrence or scalar-size bound.
The relevant ancestor follows the actual chain; preparation establishes structural and scalar prerequisites without assuming the target or dependent RE01 validity.

The following contracts are pinned at the starting revision above; their local acceptance records remain authoritative.
Their current SHA-256 values are retained in the [research manifest](../test/conformance/s06-research-manifest.json).

| Authority | Version and record | Used preconditions and disposition |
|---|---|---|
| AU | C1 v1; [AU01](content-model-s06-au-01.md) | Complete original AU/declaration sets, typed values and source contexts; universal replacement checks remain source-specific |
| PW | R-240 v1; [PW01](content-model-s06-pw-01.md) | Qualified raw placement, UPA/EDC, normalization, original bounds and #178 ranges; no new general particle proof here |
| DT | A v1; [DT01](content-model-s06-dt-01.md) | Original calendar type/facets, lexical/QName context and exact arithmetic; no calendar operand is used in the new AU theorem |

The structures XML was retrieved again from its exact pin and reproduced SHA-256 `e496af408b14853e6169ac7c1fca09d55a81bd73771758ad6be020960e1317ba`.
Final `cos-ct-extends`, `ct-props-correct`, `au-props-correct`, `derivation-ok-restriction`, `cos-particle-extend` and `cos-particle-restrict` clauses were read with deleted editorial text excluded.
The second-edition errata were checked again on October 11, 2026; E1-56 changes default/PSVI contribution, not these construction or termination obligations.

The pinned #231 guard was inspected separately through its exact Git object; no module from that draft is imported.
Bounded literature inspection found no applicable total literal/fixed-Γ procedure: current suffix-collapse implementations and schema-language inclusion papers do not establish anchored component reconstruction.
This is a limit of the evidence inspected, not an impossibility theorem or an exhaustive literature claim.

### R2: Executable literal preparation boundary

[`prepareLiteralContexts`](../test/research/re01/resolver-context.ts) consumes `ActualInput`, `Candidate` and either limits or one shared `ResolverBudget`.
It uses the existing finite graph indexing, outgoing-reference, ownership and forbidden-cycle authorities, then requires every original component in Γ to remain present.
Every original except the endpoint is reused; the endpoint preserves its identity and all facts/source metadata except its immediate base, derivation method and explicit proposed provenance.

Set-valued final/block/AU/ownership/namespace fields compare canonical identity sets; ordered content, facets, items and source contributions retain their order.
Equality of component references preserves original targets and sharing, rather than inferring equivalence from equal-looking anonymous copies.
Restriction comparison does not replace raw construction: both declared and effective content remain preserved and independently available.

Literal input supplies intermediate AU properties directly and has no D1 source actions.
The original `prepareContexts` entry retains its historical source domain and independently specified work ledger.
Literal-issued handles are authenticated separately; copied handles do not acquire that authority.

Preparation success certifies structural proposal consistency only.
The historical D1 semantic ledger returns `unresolved: literal-semantic-authorities` on these handles instead of applying its stronger inherited-AU identity rule or accepting conditional test owners as a complete literal checker.
Other results distinguish malformed input/preservation failure from sticky resource exhaustion; neither is a universal nonexistence answer.

This seam still uses the historical `Facts` vocabulary.
Fresh globally named components and incoming affiliations, complete scalar/facet qualification, all component properties, nested context certificates and every raw particle normalization are not fully represented/implemented.
Those are explicit R2 blockers, rather than a smaller replacement construction domain.

### R3: Fresh-intermediate attribute projection theorem

Let A and D have qualified complete attribute-use sets and declaration/use correctness, without presupposing their RE01 validity.
Assume literal fresh local declarations may reuse an original QName and exact simple-type identity, including references to anonymous originals without changing their original ownership.
This theorem concerns only the attribute projection of a fresh intermediate E; content, original intermediate identity and whole-context validity are separate obligations.

For every ancestor QName, construct one fresh local declaration with that QName and exactly the ancestor declaration's simple-type identity, no declaration value constraint, and one optional unconstrained use.
Give E the universal `any` attribute wildcard with `skip` processing.
No final-only QName needs a new E declaration because that wildcard admits it.

The necessary and sufficient condition is: for every QName shared by A and D, every matching D use's simple type satisfies the qualified `TypeDerivationOK(Simple)` relation to A's original simple type.
The scalar authority includes its original finals, list/union semantics and identity premises; generic graph ancestry cannot replace that relation.
The number of helper declarations and uses is at most twice the number of distinct A QNames, with no fresh scalar definition required by this projection.

Necessity follows from extension clause 1.2 and complex-type declaration uniqueness.
Every A use requires an E declaration of its QName and exact simple type; two distinct E declarations with that QName are forbidden, so D's matching restriction cannot avoid that scalar relation by choosing another E use.
Complete restriction checks apply to every matching use.

For sufficiency, E's optional unconstrained uses satisfy each extension QName/type match and impose no requiredness or fixed-value hurdle on D.
Names omitted by D may be dropped, and D-only names are wildcard-admitted; D's wildcard namespace is a subset of `any`, and its processing is at least as strong as `skip`.
E has at most one ID-derived declaration because qualified A does; all original operands remain qualified even when their constraints are absent from E.

This is a complete projection proof, not a complete full-type witness reduction.
Replacing an arbitrary witness's attributes this way can affect identity-sensitive relationships, original intermediates and other retained components, so a separate joint preservation lemma is still required.
The helper bound above cannot bound fresh particles, incoming declarations, scalar helpers or complete certificates.

[`synthesizeLiteralAu`](../test/research/re01/literal-au-synthesis.ts) executes this projection for the accepted AU probe's named `string`, `token`, `integer` and qualified `QName` domain.
It validates all original value operands before weakening any use, consumes original C1 declaration/use sets and retains original scalar labels/contexts.
An unavailable scalar/facet predicate stays unresolved; a qualified unrelated shared-name scalar yields a negative attribute projection only.

The result supplies a fresh-declaration/use plan and universal-skip wildcard, not graph identities or a complete schema receipt.
Actual declaration correctness, builtin closure and all other constraints are caller qualifications.
AU C1 and DT A are unchanged; this code uses no calendar claim or payload engine.

### Minimal fresh-helper falsifier

Take empty-content A with an optional local `g:xs:string` use fixed to 7; B restricts A by omitting g; actual D extends B with an optional local `g:xs:token` use fixed to 8.
Original A/g and D/g declarations have distinct local identities/owners; both have no declaration-level value constraint.
All types have empty final/block sets; an ordinary root refers to D, with no user affiliation, identity constraint, particle or wildcard.

Fresh E extends A using a fresh optional local `g:xs:string` declaration/use with no value constraint; proposed D restricts E and retains all of its original non-derivation properties.
E matches A's exact QName/type; token validly restricts string, and E has no fixed 7 to contradict D's fixed 8.
Using only A's original use fails the fixed-value restriction, while using only D's original use fails extension's exact simple-type identity.

The complete minimal clause inventory is the [fixed-value inventory above](#complete-minimal-fixed-value-contrast), with original A/D local scopes and token-to-string restriction added.
Local declaration names/namespaces and builtin types are legal, each type contains one non-ID declaration, empty content satisfies both steps, and E's only extension from A has a primitive possibly-vacuous RE01 discharge.
Qualified builtin/xsi closure remains a premise; this argument does not introduce a self-loop base edge.

The [independently authored graph and tests](../test/research/re01/fresh-helper.test.ts) execute the literal extension and accepted AU restriction pair, preserving original typed fixed operands and local owners.
The [synthesis controls](../test/research/re01/literal-au-synthesis.test.ts) vary incidental names/values and challenge the projection with an independent bounded scalar/requiredness/constraint matrix.
This falsifies an original-or-final-use-only specialization, not the broader F1 proposal once fresh helpers are included.

### R4: Retained obligations and a conditional well-founded measure

Retained RE01 certificates must be checked relative to their parent's coherent proposed graph.
For example, retain original X extending D vacuously in the fixed-restoration graph; after the root certificate changes D to a restriction of E, a child certificate for X must retain that proposed D and E.
Restarting X's check from original Γ loses established context facts and can create an artificial circular dependency.

A sufficient restricted certificate discipline uses no newly added nonprimitive complex extensions and overlays only targets with a proper designated ancestor A.
Here primitive means a separately qualified direct extension of that A, whose base is `anyType`; extensions with simple bases use their separate clause-2 constraints and have no clause-1.5 obligation.
Other complex extensions have rank contribution one; the explanatory note's wording that a case is trivial to check does not itself establish validity.

Overlaying a nonprimitive target changes its method to restriction; its new intermediate extends the unchanged designated A directly and is primitive, while all other existing methods and designated ancestors are preserved.
The number of nonprimitive extension components therefore strictly decreases by one, and separately qualified primitive/no-op obligations ground checking without changing their derivation to a self-loop.

This proves termination of recursively checking finite certificates satisfying that discipline, assuming each local semantic owner terminates.
It does not prove every admissible successful graph has such a certificate: the full domain allows new nonprimitive helper types, so rank may increase without a helper-elimination theorem.
It also does not bound search branching or establish terminating general negative answers.

Preparation, scalar qualification, candidate planning, helper reservations, comparisons, snapshots and final result copying share one request budget.
